People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. That's all it is.\n\n[Last Question]\nDoes this fast movement of thoughts in your mind happen every day?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 84 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear the man on the radio saying these messages, or is it more like you understand the message from what he's talking about?\n\nPatient: Well... the radio, it's just what he's saying. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages from the men at the domino table and the man on the radio are actually meant for someone else, or are you certain they're meant for you?\n\nPatient: I know they're for me. They're... they're meant for me. My wife, she says it's just talk, but... I know better. They're warning me. To be careful. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your old friend's voice?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... maybe about nine months. Off and on. The messages too... about that long. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the men at the domino table and the man on the radio are not actually sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No. I... I wouldn't have come in to see a doctor if I wasn't sure. They give these little messages. The radio announcer, those men at the table. My wife keeps saying it's just regular talk, but... no. I know a warning when I hear one. I just know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express yourself or get your thoughts across to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... sometimes. It's just... my mind, it gets going. Runs from the choir, then over to baseball, then I'm thinking about repairs around the house, then my friend... and it just goes back and forth. So fast. I can't... I can't hold onto one thought long enough to explain it right to anyone. So I just... keep quiet mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages you're hearing are real, or could they be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I thought I... I just said. They're real. I wouldn't be sitting here if it was just my own mind. The men playing dominoes... the radio announcer... they give these little messages meant for me. My wife keeps saying it's just talk, but... I know they're warning me to be careful. That's... that's all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by your mind running from one thought to another, and how that affects your daily conversations?\n\nPatient: I just... I mean, I already told you. It's just fast. Like... one minute it's the church choir, then it's baseball, then I'm thinking about fixing something at the house, then a friend... and it just loops around. So fast. I can't catch hold of it long enough to make it make sense to anybody else. So... I don't really talk about it. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Does this fast movement of thoughts in your mind happen every day?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. Most days, maybe. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me an example of what happens when you try to explain something to someone and your mind starts moving quickly from one thought to another?\n\nPatient: I don't... I can't rightly think of one. Like I said, I don't really talk about it much. If my head gets to going like that, I just... I stop trying to explain. It's too much trouble. I just keep quiet. So... no, I don't have a specific time to tell you about.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is moving quickly from one thought to another, do you find that your thoughts are logically connected or do they seem unrelated to each other?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't rightly know if they connect. Like I told you... it just runs. From the choir, to baseball, to fixing something, to my friend, and just back again. I don't guess they have much to do with each other. It's just... it all goes so fast I can't hold on to one of 'em long enough to explain it right. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you're thinking about many things at once, do you find it difficult to speak in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just told you. It goes too fast. With my mind running from the choir to baseball to fixing things... I just can't grab hold of one thought long enough to explain it right. So... yeah. I don't know about making sense. Like I said, it just runs. From the choir... to baseball... to repairs... to my friend... and back again. It's just so fast. I can't hold on to one thought long enough to even know if it makes sense to me, let alone anybody else. I just... I can't explain it right.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing, do your thoughts feel like they are connected in some way, even if they're moving quickly?\n\nPatient: I... well, I feel like I'm just repeating myself now. I really couldn't say. I don't notice any kind of tie between 'em... it's just a blur. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and thoughts are moving quickly, do you feel like you're still able to think in a somewhat logical or coherent way, even if it's hard for others to follow?\n\nPatient: I don't... I really don't know what you mean by logical. Like I just said... it's all just a blur. I can't rightly say more than that. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you're thinking about many things at once, do you feel like your thoughts are still somewhat organized, or are they completely disorganized?\n\nPatient: Look, doc... I ain't got a different answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Is your mind racing and thoughts moving quickly all the time, or are there periods when it slows down?\n\nPatient: I don't really know... I guess it ain't all the time. Sometimes it ain't so bad. But I couldn't tell you when or why it changes. It just is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your mind always racing like that, or are there times when it feels more normal?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, it ain't all the time. But when it gets to going... it just jumps. Goes from thinking about the choir... then to baseball... then to some repairs... then my friend... just back and forth. That's really all I can say about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I'm here because of the things people have been saying. The men down at the domino table, they... they say things. And sometimes the man on the radio, too. Little messages. My wife tells me it's just talk, but... I know they're meant for me. They're warning me to be careful. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear the man on the radio saying these messages, or is it more like you understand the message from what he's talking about?\n\nPatient: Well... the radio, it's just what he's saying. But... I guess I do hear things sometimes. \n\nJust... when the house is real quiet in the afternoons. I'll hear my old friend. He calls my name from out on the porch... or hums a choir song. It's real clear. Just like he's standing right there. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see anything that isn't really there, like your old friend on the porch?\n\nPatient: No... no, I don't see him. I just... I just hear him. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages from the men at the domino table and the man on the radio are actually meant for someone else, or are you certain they're meant for you?\n\nPatient: I know they're for me. They're... they're meant for me. My wife, she says it's just talk, but... I know better. They're warning me. To be careful. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your old friend's voice?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... maybe about nine months. Off and on. The messages too... about that long. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the men at the domino table and the man on the radio are not actually sending you messages?\n\nPatient: No. I... I wouldn't have come in to see a doctor if I wasn't sure. They give these little messages. The radio announcer, those men at the table. My wife keeps saying it's just regular talk, but... no. I know a warning when I hear one. I just know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express yourself or get your thoughts across to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... sometimes. It's just... my mind, it gets going. Runs from the choir, then over to baseball, then I'm thinking about repairs around the house, then my friend... and it just goes back and forth. So fast. I can't... I can't hold onto one thought long enough to explain it right to anyone. So I just... keep quiet mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages you're hearing are real, or could they be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I thought I... I just said. They're real. I wouldn't be sitting here if it was just my own mind. The men playing dominoes... the radio announcer... they give these little messages meant for me. My wife keeps saying it's just talk, but... I know they're warning me to be careful. That's... that's all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by your mind running from one thought to another, and how that affects your daily conversations?\n\nPatient: I just... I mean, I already told you. It's just fast. Like... one minute it's the church choir, then it's baseball, then I'm thinking about fixing something at the house, then a friend... and it just loops around. So fast. I can't catch hold of it long enough to make it make sense to anybody else. So... I don't really talk about it. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Does this fast movement of thoughts in your mind happen every day?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. Most days, maybe. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me an example of what happens when you try to explain something to someone and your mind starts moving quickly from one thought to another?\n\nPatient: I don't... I can't rightly think of one. Like I said, I don't really talk about it much. If my head gets to going like that, I just... I stop trying to explain. It's too much trouble. I just keep quiet. So... no, I don't have a specific time to tell you about.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is moving quickly from one thought to another, do you find that your thoughts are logically connected or do they seem unrelated to each other?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't rightly know if they connect. Like I told you... it just runs. From the choir, to baseball, to fixing something, to my friend, and just back again. I don't guess they have much to do with each other. It's just... it all goes so fast I can't hold on to one of 'em long enough to explain it right. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you're thinking about many things at once, do you find it difficult to speak in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just told you. It goes too fast. With my mind running from the choir to baseball to fixing things... I just can't grab hold of one thought long enough to explain it right. So... yeah. I reckon I don't make much sense to folks when it gets like that.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you're thinking about many things, do your thoughts still seem to make sense to you, even if others can't follow them?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't know about making sense. Like I said, it just runs. From the choir... to baseball... to repairs... to my friend... and back again. It's just so fast. I can't hold on to one thought long enough to even know if it makes sense to me, let alone anybody else. I just... I can't explain it right.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing, do your thoughts feel like they are connected in some way, even if they're moving quickly?\n\nPatient: I... well, I feel like I'm just repeating myself now. I really couldn't say. I don't notice any kind of tie between 'em... it's just a blur. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and thoughts are moving quickly, do you feel like you're still able to think in a somewhat logical or coherent way, even if it's hard for others to follow?\n\nPatient: I don't... I really don't know what you mean by logical. Like I just said... it's all just a blur. I can't rightly say more than that. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you're thinking about many things at once, do you feel like your thoughts are still somewhat organized, or are they completely disorganized?\n\nPatient: Look, doc... I don't know how else to tell ya. Organized, disorganized... I just don't know. Like I keep saying, it all just goes too fast to tell. I ain't got a different answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Is your mind racing and thoughts moving quickly all the time, or are there periods when it slows down?\n\nPatient: I don't really know... I guess it ain't all the time. Sometimes it ain't so bad. But I couldn't tell you when or why it changes. It just is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your mind always racing like that, or are there times when it feels more normal?\n\nPatient: Like I just said, it ain't all the time. But when it gets to going... it just jumps. Goes from thinking about the choir... then to baseball... then to some repairs... then my friend... just back and forth. It moves so fast I can't hold onto one thought long enough to explain it right to you. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often have difficulty understanding what you are trying to communicate when you are talking to them?\n\nPatient: I couldn't really say... ain't nobody told me they don't understand me. I reckon folks get what I'm saying just fine. I don't do a whole lot of talking to people anyway.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
